### Content

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM We’re giving away a limited number of exclusive Jobber Forum sticker packs to members who introduce themselves! To enter, answer the following in the thread below 👇 Your name Your business name and industry How many years have you been in business? What is your favorite thing about being a business owner? 🎁 Once you’ve posted your intro, fill out this quick form so we can mail you your stickers* 👉 https://forms.gle/WjgAEC1WwY37ZbGq8 *stickers will only be sent to participants Missed the giveaway? Keep your eye out in the forum for future giveaways! Sticker packs are free and available while supplies last!

## 3. April Community Spotlight: Powered by Real Experience

**Author:** julie (Jobber Community Team)
**Posted:** 2026-05-01
**Views:** 44 | **Likes:** 2 | **Comments:** 1
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/announcements/april-community-spotlight-powered-by-real-experience/10694

### Content

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM This month had a recurring thread running through the best conversations: what it actually costs to run a business: in time, money, and everything in between. These five members had something real to say about it. 👇 MoisesCabriales — for not being afraid to ask. Questions "How to get HVAC customers when starting out with little to no marketing budget?" are exactly the convos more people are thinking about but not starting. Keep them coming. michael090 — for bringing sharp, actionable advice without the fluff. Their breakdown of how to push margins from 20% to 30% and their reframing lead struggles as a follow-up system problem are the kind of responses that make threads worth reading twice. MendenhallCtrg — for starting one of the most substantive threads this month. Their post on pricing from actual business costs (not competitor rates) and their in the parental leave thread was the kind of honest, been-there answer that's hard to come by. OdHoneyBee — for the most unexpected AI use case of the month. Using AI for bee species identification to cut down on misrouted calls is a great reminder that the best tools solve your specific problem, not someone else's. StandingonGrace — for jumping in with both feet. From sharing a practical take on quoting in the cleaning industry to opening up about the personal story behind starting Crown Dominion Cleaning, they're bringing the kind of range that makes a community feel real. Drop a comment to give these members some love — and if you've been sitting on a question or a story, this is your sign to post it. 👇

### Replies (1)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** Riseunbrokenco
**Posted:** 2026-05-03

First of all, I commend you all for having the courage to have the guts to start a business. Whether it be your heart and your soul or a higher power or your faith or just your gut telling you that you believe in yourself enough to pursue, it is showing something amazing. I’m working on the blueprint and the blooming of the foundation as well I look up to people all of you that give me opportunity to learn every single day how to utilize my strengths and flourish them by not stealing others ideas but looking at what has worked for them and what they truly believe in I feet off of real feelings and each of these people really seem to put their heart and soul into it. Congratulations.

## 6. Never Miss an Expert Answer: Here's Every Ask-an-Expert We've Ever Done

**Author:** julie (Jobber Community Team)
**Posted:** 2026-05-01
**Views:** 7 | **Likes:** 0 | **Comments:** 1
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/announcements/never-miss-an-expert-answer-heres-every-ask-an-expert-weve-ever-done/10691

### Content

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM If you're new here (or just haven't explored much yet), our Ask-an-Expert sessions are one of the most valuable things we run in this community. Here's how it works: we bring in a real expert, someone who works specifically with home service business owners, and open the floor for one week. **Author:** MarcA
**Posted:** 2026-04-14

JOBBER PRODUCT TEAM to bigzack1986 bigzack1986​ - absolutely! Here are way Jobber helps get you new clients: Websites are a critical in building your online presence and credibility. Jobber has an AI-powered website creation tool that uses your company data already in Jobber (location, services offered, etc.) to build a multi-page, SEO optimized website in just minutes. And it is free to all Jobber customers in all base Jobber plans! This will help you get found by customers looking for your services. Most customers use Google to search for services, so it is important to put your best foot forward with your Google Business Profile to attract new clients. In Jobber you can connect your GBP in just a couple of steps (or we help you create one if you need to do that first) - once connected, Jobber will optimize it based on all your critical business data. You can also connect your online booking form to your GBP so when they find you, they can book you right away without leaving the page! Reviews are critical when customers are selecting a provider. Jobber Marketing Suite include the Reviews Tool, which can be set up to automatically request a Google review after each job. That way you never miss an opportunity to get your next 5-star review! Many of our customers double their review count using this feature, and 5-star reviews automatically publish to your GBP! Good old word-of-mouth is still a great way to find new customers. Jobber's Marketing Suite has something called Referrals, which allows you to set up an automatic referral program that incents your happy customers to pass on your name to others. When a new customer books using their personalized referral link, your existing customer gets $ or a % of their next job. So it keeps them coming back too! Does that help?

**Author:** StephenJobe
**Posted:** 2026-04-10

to xueming Not currently. But a great way to do that would be to utilize the time while you are "inputing payments" to have them scan the code and leave a review. It takes literally 5 seconds and most people are willing to do that since it's dead space while they wait. Having the QR code on a card or even the back of your phone for them to scan as you take the payment is a great way to efficiently do so. ALSO, we emailed all of clients and made the subject: "How do I ask for a Google Review from you?" (or something that) and then invited everyone to leave a review if they hadn't done so. From that alone, we've had some substantial daily moves. Though this is a one time move so make sure its a good one if you are going to use it.

TERMS & CONDITIONS Video estimates are offered for select plumbing services where all work areas are clearly visible and accessible during the call. Video estimates do not apply to active leaks, diagnostic services, underground plumbing, re-pipes, or work requiring wall or concrete access. **Posted:** 2026-04-13

Also worth previewing the invoice before sending to confirm it’s showing up where you expect. If it’s still not appearing, it might be worth reaching out to Jobber support—sometimes it’s tied to how the invoice template is configured.

## 11. Home Service Pros, This is Your Era. But Are You Taking Advantage of it?

**Author:** julie (Jobber Community Team)
**Posted:** 2026-04-09
**Views:** 100 | **Likes:** 2 | **Comments:** 3
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/announcements/home-service-pros-this-is-your-era-but-are-you-taking-advantage-of-it/9736

### Content

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM This is the era of the blue collar business owner. Demand is strong, job sizes are growing, and the economy needs you more than ever. BUT there's a widening gap between businesses that are pulling ahead and those just keeping up, and it comes down to the tools they use. 88% of fully-booked, high-growth businesses are using AI in their day-to-day operations. Only 27% of struggling ones are. 64% of pros under 30 are already using AI and they're applying it across invoicing (61%), quoting (55%), marketing (55%), and more. 👉 A new 2026 Home Service Trends Report breaks down exactly what top performers are doing differently: pricing, lead gen, response times, and more. Where do you think your business falls? Are you keeping up, or pulling ahead?

### Replies (3)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** PestFreeCanada
**Posted:** 2026-04-09

What would be a good way to fond what AI processes could be working for your business? I don't see any use for it in my day to day but I could massively be missing out on some opportunity by not knowing what I don't know.

#### Reply 2

**Author:** jrselectric
**Posted:** 2026-04-09

In our electrical business, we’ve started using AI and automation to tighten up the backend—things faster quoting, cleaner proposals, and more consistent follow-ups. It’s not replacing the work, but it’s making us way more efficient and professional on the customer side. Response time alone has been a game changer. That said, there’s still a gap. The companies really winning right now are the ones fully integrating AI into estimating, marketing, and operations, not just dabbling in it. That’s the next level we’re actively building toward. Bottom line—if you’re not using tools to move faster and look more polished than your competition, you’re probably just keeping up… and in this market, that’s risky.

#### Reply 3

**Author:** ZacRadcat
**Posted:** 2026-04-09

We have used multiple AI systems including the pro version of GPT. Once trained, AI can quickly dig through repetitive grunt work and save a ton of time. But, that is just the tip of the ice berg. At this point, nearly every component of our company is using some form of AI and as we continue to grow that is only going to increase.

**Author:** jonmaegaard
**Posted:** 2026-02-09

to PestFreeCanada Hey Daniel, A vacation would be great. We have to get away from the Great White North now and then to warm up! You are not alone with the nerves of expansion. It has been something I have wrestled with too. For myself I think pride has gotten in the way of expansion. No one will do it me, they will not treat the client the same, they will be **bleep** the equipment etc.(although I have wrecked some of my own by accident ....ooops). Then confidence gets in the way. Now I would have to manage people, find more work, buy more equipment and make some more excuses on why not :) I think a big consideration is when do you get some needed down time as a Solo. Burn out state is no bueno. What does that look for you? Can you take a week or two off to regroup? For my self I am just seasonal in Edmonton. Start in April and work 5- 6 days a week till the the first snow fall October / November. Then take the winter off. So for the summer there are no breaks and I cant get hurt or sick. **Author:** ValliExcavation
**Posted:** 2026-03-09

to PestFreeCanada Hey Daniel! This is an incredibly common mindset- I can assure you that you aren't alone. Many people start their own business because they want to do things their own way without having someone bossing them around. You might surprise yourself seeing how much time you spend on doing the tedious back end tasks.

**Author:** QC-Handyman-Co
**Posted:** 2026-03-09

I just started my business this month after years as a real estate agent and seeing a big hole that needed to be filled in that industry. A reliable, trustworthy handyman who can show up to a property quickly and give a clean, line-by-line quote for the items that need fixed or replaced and, if requested, have the issues repaired in a timely manner so to not jeopardize the transaction. As a real estate agent, I realized how difficult it was to find someone willing to do this and have the quote returned quickly. Now onto the goals. Q1 Goals: -Stay consistent with marketing, -Track every job, -Track time on site, -Track drive time, -Track Admin time, -track hourly effective rate. These are important goals because I've never done them. This will build the foundation of where I am and where I should set my goals. That being said, I do have some goals in mind already. 1. 9am-5pm M-F only (I'm willing to make some exceptions for good clients in an emergency). While we all need to work, losing quality time with my family and friends shouldn't happen. 2. Tighten my service radius. I'm doing smaller jobs that are too far away because I'm a new business and am hungry for work. The last job I did I had an effective labor rate of $44/hour before expenses. Simply not enough for before expenses. This this goal is 10 jobs within 20 minutes of my house (office). Of those 10 jobs, I'd to obtain a 50% review rate. As for a Q1 dollar amount, I'm shooting for $9k-$11k. Q2 Goals: 1. Revenue Target: $24k-$30k. 2. 30+ total reviews. 3. 4 total realtor relationships (that are producing at least 2 jobs per month). 5. Be one week booked out (other than the time I've set aside for realtor quote jobs). Q3 Goals: 1. Revenue Target: $30k-$36k. 2. Increase minimum service call if demand supports it. 3. Maintain 50%+ net margin. 4. 60+ total reviews. 5. 30% of jobs from repeat/referrals. 6. Be consistently booked 1-2 weeks out. Q4 Goals: 1. Revenue Target: $30k-$40k. 2. 100+ Total Reviews. 3. 40% of jobs from repeat/referrals. 4. Realtors sending me steady small repair work. 5. Be in a place where I need to decide if I'm going to stay a 1 person business or add part-time help for 2027 scale. All of that aside, here is a TLDR simplified: -I want to be averaging $7,500 per month by June 30. -Work 4-5 job days per week (no more) -by the end of the year, be booked out consistently for 1-2 weeks

**Author:** kasyallen
**Posted:** 2026-02-09

Hello Jobber Community! I’m really looking forward to this one. I had the chance to speak last year, and if I’m being honest, I spent more time preparing and speaking than actually enjoying the experience. This year? I’m soaking it all in and can’t wait to hear from so many great speakers. If I had to pick one I’m especially excited about, it’s Marc Randolph. We heard him speak, and his message absolutely lit a fire under how we think about hiring... and letting go. One line in particular stuck with me: “Stop telling me your company is a family. It’s not. Just think for a moment: when was the last time you fired someone from your family?” Oof. But also… spot on. It’s your team, not your family. He also challenged leaders to look at their current team and ask a tough question: If you had the chance today, would you hire them again? That’s a tall drink of water - especially for small teams - but it’s a powerful lens for both hiring and ongoing performance conversations. Highly recommend the read if you haven’t seen it yet: https://mbrandolph.medium.com/you-are-a-team-not-a-family-7d4b626c4de8 P.S. Thank you, Julie !

**Views:** 85 | **Likes:** 3 | **Comments:** 1
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/announcements/share-your-experience-with-jobber-and-get-a-20-gift-card/7087

### Content

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM We’d love to hear from you! As we've seen many times on the Home Service Community, you rely on real feedback from peers when choosing the right tools. By sharing your experience in a review, you’ll help others in the industry make more informed decisions—and as a thank you, you’ll receive a $20 USD prepaid Mastercard. Here’s how it works: Leave a review of Jobber on TrustRadius (it usually takes about 10 minutes). Once your review is published (within about 48 hours), TrustRadius will send you a confirmation email with the subject line: “TrustRadius has published your review.” Post a screenshot of that confirmation email here in this thread 👀 We’ll send your $20 gift card to the email connected to your HSC account. If you’d it sent to a different email, just note that in your post. Rewards will be sent within 7 days. LEAVE A REVIEW A few important details: This opportunity is open until October 1st, 2025 The first 30 published reviews will qualify for the $20 gift card. You can choose to publish anonymously if you’d . This offer is open only to those who haven’t left a TrustRadius review for Jobber before. Thanks for lending your voice—your review can make a real difference for other pros in the industry! SUCCESS STORIES

**Author:** MrBackflow
**Posted:** 2025-07-02

We got a 90/100... ( As expected ) This month I’m laser-focusing on one thing: 🚀 cranking our Google-review count from 32 to 50+. Why that move? • It’s the quickest way to widen our digital “word-of-mouth” reach and edge out the 30-plus nearby competitors. • More recent, detailed reviews = higher map-pack ranking and instant trust. How I’ll do it (30-day sprint): 1. Drop a friendly SMS + email the day after every service with a direct review link. 2. Stick a QR-code “Rate Your Flow” card on every invoice and service tag. 3. Run a mini “Clean Water, Clean Karma” promo: leave a review, get $10 off next annual test. 4. Track progress in GBP Insights weekly and celebrate the team when we hit milestones. Simple, measurable, high-impact—that’s the play for June. 💧

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM We’ve got something exciting for you! We’re giving away 3 Jobber Swag Boxes 🎁 🎁 🎁 Want in? **Author:** Go2Girl
**Posted:** 2025-04-28

Automations: Can automatically tell me (or client) when the next available booking is in their area, for the service requested, length of time, etc. Not sure if this is considered an automation but - it's fantastic that we can email clients via jobber however it's annoying that there's not a two-way email system ( the two-way text system) in Jobber. I have to go to an exterior email address, and either respond in there or copy from email and paste the conversation in Jobber's email to respond etc. Inventory - tracking & reordering Cancellations - if a client needs to cancel, being able to cancel themselves. If my business has cancellation policies, those policies would also be automatically implemented such as cancellation fees. Would be nice too if they need to reschedule, the system could reschedule the clients. Client portal - It would be convenient to have everything in one place. The contracts they signed, upcoming and past appointments, photos my employees uploaded to their profile, all invoices, etc. Recurring jobs ending soon - A notification of some sort to the client asking if they want to renew/talk about renewing, and a notification on my end so that I know their contract is expiring, without having to remember to search for it or create tasks on the calendar etc. Replacing responsibid as Mckunz suggested below. I can't recall the name gps tracking program that you mentioned would be a new feature this spring. I was super excited when I was watching the announcement feed as it had features I needed, but disappointed to find out that it's not offered in Canada. I'd love to have a way to include employee availability and time off requests etc. I have so much more to write lol - but I have to get back to work!!

## 31. Want to be featured in Jobber's Home Service Economic Report? 👀

**Author:** julie (Jobber Community Team)
**Posted:** 2025-02-28
**Views:** 231 | **Likes:** 2 | **Comments:** 2
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/announcements/want-to-be-featured-in-jobbers-home-service-economic-report-%F0%9F%91%80/2002

### Content

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM Hi everyone! 👋 Our marketing team is sourcing insights from home service pros to capture in an upcoming Jobber Home Service Economic Report. 🙌 You don't need to be a Jobber customer to participate. 🌟 If you'd to potentially be featured in the report, please DM me with responses to as many of the following questions as you feel comfortable answering: Was it more challenging to book new work in 2024 compared to 2023? Please explain. Did you increase your prices in 2024? If so, by how much? What was your biggest challenge in 2024? Please explain. What do you predict will be your biggest challenge in 2025? Please explain. Do you expect your revenue to increase, decrease, or stay the same in 2025? Please explain. Do you have a positive, negative, or neutral outlook for the next 6-12 months? Please explain. Do you anticipate your pricing strategy to change in the next 6 months? Please explain. Is there anything you'd to share about your 2024 business performance or 2025 outlook? 🌟 Please include your name (if different from your profile), business name, location, industry, and email address in your DM. A marketing team member will follow up with you via email with the next steps! Thank you!

#### Reply 11

**Author:** ameliad
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to jsa2025 Hi. We email reminders 2 times (2 weeks), then a bit firmer of a response from a senior accounting member giving 3 days to respond, call once and if no luck we send the invoice with a letter via Canada Post (well when they are not on strike) with a deadline to respond and I have had a lot of success with this.

#### Reply 12

**Author:** DavidEyerly
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to jsa2025 Your persistence in following up with leads shows dedication, but it’s important to strike a balance between persistence and respecting a potential client’s boundaries. Let’s break this down: 1. Understanding the Nature of the Lead How Was the Lead Generated? The origin of the lead matters. If the lead came through a referral, a direct inquiry, or a conversation where they expressed genuine interest, a bit more persistence is acceptable. However, if it came through a less direct method— a web inquiry or a cold outreach campaign—they may not have strong interest, and excessive follow-ups could harm your reputation. Qualification of the Lead: Before pursuing a lead aggressively, assess how "qualified" they are. If they haven’t shown significant engagement, their silence may indicate they’re not ready to commit. 2. Respect Their Silence If a lead isn’t responding, it could mean several things: they’re busy, uninterested, or their priorities have changed. Continuing to call repeatedly without a response may come off as pushy and turn them off from working with you in the future. Think of your follow-ups as providing opportunities rather than applying pressure. A couple of polite attempts at different times (e.g., an initial call, a follow-up email or text a few days later) should be sufficient. If they’re interested, they’ll respond. 3. Adopt a Multi-Channel Approach Rather than relying solely on calls, consider sending a professional email or text. Sometimes people are more comfortable responding in writing or outside of business hours. If they still don’t respond, you can leave the door open with a final message: “I understand this might not be the right time for you. If your needs change, please don’t hesitate to reach out. Wishing you the best!” This leaves a good impression without being overly persistent. 4. Timeframe for Follow-Ups A general rule of thumb is to make 2-3 attempts within a week or two, spaced appropriately. After that, it’s better to move on and focus on more promising leads. You can revisit cold leads after a few months if you think the timing might have been an issue. 5. Why Over-Persistence Can Backfire Repeated attempts can come across as desperate or unprofessional. They may also damage your reputation if the lead shares their negative experience with others. Time spent chasing uninterested leads is time you could use to build relationships with genuinely interested clients or to market yourself to new prospects. 6. A Strategy for the Future Consider implementing a lead-nurturing system where you track interactions and schedule a limited number of follow-ups for each lead. Automating emails or texts for non-responding leads could save time and reduce the risk of over-persistence. I once used "SalesForce" but it was overly complex for our little business, and ludicrously expensive. My sales people hated it! Make it easy for potential clients to say “no.” Some people simply don’t respond because they’re uncomfortable rejecting someone directly. You know how you dislike unwanted e-mail. So do they. Most legitimate marketing provide an "opt out" button. Do the same for your leads: Provide them with a polite way to decline if they’re not interested, such as, “If this isn’t a good fit for you right now, just let us know, and we won’t bother you further.” Closing Thought: Your time and energy are valuable. It’s better spent on qualified, engaged leads than on chasing silent ones. Persistence is important, but professionalism and respect for a potential client’s time and preferences are what will leave the lasting impression.

#### Reply 27

**Author:** Adamslawncare
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

In landscaping and hardscaping how to you get more sales in the winter? I’ve tried Christmas lights and that never sells due to pricing. So how do you keep business flowing in the down season?

#### Reply 28

**Author:** DavidEyerly
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to Adamslawncare I have a dumpster rental business. We have the same issue--winter is not a good time for folks to clean out their homes. For us, we've just decided it is reality, and we use the down-time to vacation! That works for us, but not for everyone. Here are some more practical suggestions we've worked out in our business: 1. Offer Winter Services Snow Removal: This is a classic service in many areas during the winter. You can offer snow plowing, salting, and shoveling for both residential and commercial properties. It’s a recurring service that can bring in steady income during the colder months. Winterizing Lawns and Gardens: Offer packages that include preparing yards for winter, such as aerating, fertilizing, and winterizing irrigation systems. This can also include protecting plants and trees from frost or cold damage. 2. Focus on Hardscaping Projects Winter Hardscaping: Winter can be a great time for installing patios, retaining walls, and other hardscaping features. The ground can still be worked, and customers may have more time to invest in these larger projects since they are less concerned about the state of their lawns during the colder months. Fire Pits and Outdoor Heating: Many homeowners invest in fire pits, outdoor kitchens, and space heaters in the winter to extend the use of their outdoor living areas. Offering these products can provide additional sales in the colder months. 3. Offer Landscaping Design and Planning Winter Design Consultations: Use the slow season to offer consultations for landscaping design projects that will be implemented in the spring. You can promote landscaping plans, garden designs, and hardscaping layouts to be executed once the weather warms up. Customers may want to plan ahead, and winter can be a great time to book projects for the upcoming season. 4. Sell Landscaping Products Winter Garden Supplies: Sell items such as decorative planters, Christmas trees, firewood, or holiday-themed garden decorations. Offering home delivery for these products can keep sales going through the season. Gift Cards or Pre-Orders: Offering gift cards or pre-order options for landscaping services can help secure income. Customers who purchase these items as gifts or for future services will ensure business continuity. 5. Expand into Other Services Holiday Light Installation: While you mentioned that Christmas light installations haven’t sold due to pricing, you might consider offering more affordable, smaller-scale packages, such as small roofline decorations or path lights. This could make it more affordable and appealing to a wider customer base. Gutter Cleaning or Roof Maintenance: These services are essential in the winter, particularly in regions with heavy snow or ice. Offering gutter cleaning, roof inspections, or ice dam removal can keep cash flow steady. 6. Use the Off-Season for Marketing Targeted Promotions: Offer early-bird specials or discounts for spring services. This encourages customers to book and pay for services in advance, helping to generate revenue during the slower months. Strengthen Your Online Presence: Winter is a good time to invest in building or refining your website, social media profiles, and digital marketing strategies. Engaging with customers through informative blog posts, social media updates, or email newsletters can keep your brand top of mind when spring rolls around. At Griffin Waste we do this a lot--it is our "winter job" (and we can do it while vacationing!) 7. Seasonal Maintenance Contracts Offer annual maintenance contracts that include snow removal, yard cleanup, and winterizing services, with a monthly or quarterly payment plan. This provides consistent cash flow throughout the year and keeps customers committed. 8. Offer Education and Workshops Host virtual workshops or webinars about winter care for landscapes or hardscaping maintenance. Charge a fee for these sessions or use them as an opportunity to upsell services, such as winterizing services, design consultations, or product sales. 9. Leverage Partnerships Collaborate with Other Businesses: Team up with local home improvement stores, holiday decorators, or construction companies to offer bundled services or cross-promotions. For instance, offering hardscaping services in collaboration with a pool company could create year-round interest. 10. Network and Build Relationships Build Relationships with Property Managers: If you work with commercial or residential property managers, they may have opportunities to provide services snow removal, seasonal cleanup, or winter maintenance for larger properties, which may have ongoing needs during the off-season. Good luck to you--when it was just me, it was easy just to take the time off. But now I have to keep the employees busy in order to keep them on payroll.

**Author:** Scottief
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

Is there a way to make routes in maps so it can show you the fastest route for a day of your jobs?

#### Reply 32

**Author:** DavidEyerly
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to Scottief Scottief, Many companies Amazon, UPS, and FedEx use advanced routing software that helps them optimize delivery routes, considering factors traffic, distance, and delivery time windows. For smaller businesses, there are several online mapping tools that provide route optimization features, and some can be integrated into systems Jobber. Here are some options that might work for your needs: Google Maps API – While Google Maps may not provide multi-stop route optimization out of the box for end users, its API does support optimization features for multiple locations. You can integrate Google Maps with systems Jobber through custom coding or use third-party tools to implement it. However, there may be a cost depending on usage. RouteXL – A free-to-use (with limitations) service that allows you to plan multi-stop routes. You can enter up to 20 locations for free, and it will optimize the route for you. There's a paid version if you need more stops. You could potentially integrate it with Jobber or similar systems through API calls. MapQuest – MapQuest offers route optimization for multiple locations and has both free and paid plans. It also provides an API that could be integrated into Jobber, allowing you to optimize routes for deliveries. Route Planning from TruckRouter – This is a web-based service specifically designed for optimizing truck routes, but it can be used for our purposes. It has a free version for limited functionality and could be used for route planning. Circuit Route Planner – A route optimization app that offers a free plan for up to 10 stops. It also offers a paid version for larger route optimization, but you can start with the free version. Circuit offers an API for integration with other platforms, and it may integrate well with Jobber or other scheduling systems. Waze – Waze offers routing with real-time traffic updates, which can be an advantage for route optimization. While not traditionally for businesses, you could use Waze to get live information that could help in adjusting routes. However, integrating Waze directly into systems Jobber would require custom development. Many of these services also offer APIs that allow businesses to integrate their routing features directly into other software platforms Jobber. Some of these APIs are free or low-cost for moderate usage, so you should be able to find a solution that fits your budget. For those of you that don't know, an API is a set of rules and protocols that defines how software components interact. For example, an API might allow one program to request information from another program ( retrieving the fastest route from Google Maps). You'll need someone that knows how to integrate an API to make it work in Jobber, or we can ask Jobber to provide us this useful feature.

**Author:** DavidEyerly
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to LuckyMelDoc First, I want to say how incredibly sorry I am that you’ve had to endure such a relentless series of challenges. No one deserves that level of stress, and it’s completely understandable to feel frustrated, betrayed, and disillusioned by the systems that are supposed to protect small business owners you. Your perseverance in rebuilding everything despite all of this speaks volumes about your strength and resilience. I've rarely had these issues. I run a dumpster rental business. Some calls us, orders a dumpster, we put it in their driveway, invoice immediately, and then pick the dumpster up when they say, "It's full." I thought when I bought this business, "what could go wrong!" However, someone once rented a dumpster from us, we delivered it, and they paid with a stolen credit card. Immediately Quickbooks (which we integrate into Jobber) flagged us that the credit card was stolen. Soon thereafter, someone called us to ask us why the number on our dumpster wasn't the one in our "for sale" ad. "What for sale ad?" we asked. Seems that the person who fraudulently rented the dumpster put up a for sale listing on Facebook Marketplace SELLING our dumpster! Had we not gotten alerted by the hopeful buyer, we would have lost a dumpster. Who steals dumpsters? Anyway, I just want you to know that, unfortunately you’re not alone—many small business owners have faced similar hardships, though maybe not all at once as you have. Scams, fraudulent lawsuits, employee theft, and identity theft have become more prevalent, and it often feels as if the law protects the wrong people. That said, there are steps other small businesses have taken to try to reduce their risk moving forward. You’ve already implemented some smart moves— installing security systems and freezing your credit reports—so I want to share a few other ideas that might help: Consult a Business Fraud Specialist: There are professionals who specialize in preventing and addressing business scams, including those targeting your online presence. They may have tools to help you reclaim your company’s digital identity and offer strategies to secure your brand name. Legal Shield Services: Consider legal subscription services ( LegalShield or similar) that provide ongoing access to lawyers for a more affordable monthly fee. They can assist with fraud cases, fake lawsuits, and employee issues. Network with Other Small Business Owners: You’re not alone in these struggles, and connecting with other local small business owners—through chambers of commerce, industry associations, or online forums—might give you valuable insights or even leads on resources you haven’t tried yet. Publicize Your Experience to Build Awareness: Sharing your story (through local media, business blogs, or your own company’s website) might not only warn others but could rally community support and potentially shine a light on the need for policy changes. Work with a Reputable CPA or Tax Advocate: Regarding the Paycom situation, a Certified Public Accountant (CPA) or tax advocate can sometimes mediate disputes with employment offices and payroll services. They may also help determine whether legal recourse is worth pursuing. Finally, you’re absolutely right—society should do more to support small businesses yours. Small, family-run businesses are the backbone of our communities, and it’s incredibly disheartening when they’re the ones left unprotected. I hope sharing your experience helps others realize just how serious this issue is and encourages small business owners to band together for change. Please know that your struggles matter, and your resilience hasn’t gone unnoticed. I hope better days are ahead for you and your company. If there’s any way this group can support you further, please don’t hesitate to ask. You’ve been through more than enough on your own. Take care, and keep fighting the good fight!

#### Reply 47

**Author:** MHalv
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

Hey there, How many days after you send the initial invoice is it okay to send a friendly reminder?

#### Reply 48

**Author:** DavidEyerly
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to MHalv This answer may not suit every situation--and it depends quite a bit on your customers. We require payment when the project starts. But, Griffin Waste is a dumpster rental business--so when we drop off the dumpster, we invoice immediately. The customer is now happy the dumpster has been delivered, and we send an invoice through jobber by text. They pay us often before we've left their driveway! For most businesses, and for any net 30 customer, it’s generally a good idea to send a friendly reminder 7 to 10 days after the initial invoice if the payment hasn’t been received or acknowledged. This timeframe strikes a balance between giving the client sufficient time to process the invoice and ensuring it stays on their radar. If your payment terms are shorter (e.g., pay upon receipt, net 7 or net 14), you might send a reminder a little sooner, around the 5-7 day mark. For longer terms (e.g., net 30), a reminder at the 10-14 day point works well. The key is to keep the tone light and polite—sometimes payments just get overlooked! Also, if there is any time, have the friendliest person on your staff give them a call. If it is truly, "Oh, I forgot," well you just reminded them. A written invoice is very official--a friendly call is just a nice discussion that might start, "Hey, did everything get done to your satisfaction? Oh, and . . . we've not been paid yet" comes up later in the conversation.

**Author:** LuckyMelDoc
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to CyberFox It is the most amazing, life-changing site on the web for those of us in the trades. It replaces tons and tons of office staff, thus streamlining your business and saving you time and money. I used to have three secretaries in my office handling bookings, billing, quotes, etc. Quotes and appointments were getting lost, some by accident and some on purpose. No one knew who had sent what so some customers were getting double billed or double booked and no one ever knew who told what customer what info. It was sheer chaos! On top of all that mess, our workers were skimming money and doing drugs when unsupervised. Worse yet, we caught one worker red-handed stealing customer addresses, phone numbers, and appointment information. They would send the info to their friend who would then take over our jobs and take the down payment money while pretending to be us. However, they wouldn't complete the jobs! We were not wise to the tricks since the calenders and files were always a big mess anyhow and the employee in question would make sure all the customer info was deleted from our accounts. I was racing all around trying to clean up messes and divert disaster while trying to run the office. We looked really bad since jobs were going unfinished, payments were being doubled, and employees were drugged up. It was a literal train wreck! I was missing so much of my kid's lives while babysitting my staff. I finally threw in the towel at the end of 2020 and fired everyone! I got Jobber and never looked back! Jobber doesn't get drugged up, doesn't steal my customers, and doesn't skim money off the top of my bottom line! Furthermore, I don't have to babysit Jobber it is some toddler needing its Mommy 24/7! Jobber keeps my jobs straight and secure, collects the money owed to me, keeps my quotes organized, keeps my customer's information locked up and safe, and Jobber keeps my customers informed of their appointments! I am now doing the work of 4 people in less than half of the time it took me to run around a crazy person when I had employees mucking up the works! I therefore have more time for my family and friends. Everything takes far less time with Jobber because everything is organized and in one place. Less time means less labor and less labor means less overhead costs. It is pure genius! My accountant even said that minus all the employee disasters we are now making more money. That in itself is a huge testament to Jobber's worth. From setting the appointment to sending the quote to doing the job, collecting payment, and getting feedback from the customer, it is all done right through Jobber. It has saved my sanity and my business! I love, love, love Jobber!!
